=== 154 ===
"Then after the distress He sent down upon you security — drowsiness overcoming a faction of you — while another faction cared only about themselves, thinking of Allah other than the truth, the thought of ignorance, saying, 'Is there anything for us in this matter?' Say, 'Indeed, the matter belongs entirely to Allah.' They conceal within themselves what they do not reveal to you. They say, 'If there was anything we could do in the matter, we would not have been killed here.' Say, 'Even if you had been in your houses, those for whom killing was decreed would have gone out to the places of their death.' And that Allah might test what is in your breasts and purify what is in your hearts. And Allah is Knowing of that within the breasts."

After ghamm, security: a safe sleep that fell on a group of believers — sakinah in the form of nu'as — while another group cared for themselves, thinking of Allah the thought of Jahiliyyah: is there anything for us in this amr? Say: the whole amr is Allah's. They hide what they do not show you. They say if we had a say we would not have been killed here. Say: even in your homes, those decreed to be killed would have gone out to their death beds. He tests what is in breasts and purifies hearts. Knowing of the breasts.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, and companions among the true slept a security sleep while hypocrites stayed agitated with conspiracy thought. He taught qadr of death and forbade the Jahiliyyah claim that death is only bad planning. Testing breasts and purifying hearts is the aim under the trauma.

Two groups under one distress: one receives sakinah; one cooks accusation. Check which group you join after communal pain. Sleep of the truthful is a gift; insomnia of the selfish is a sign. Amr all for Allah ends the fantasy that your committee would have rewritten death.

Even in your houses — the decree brings the destined out to their madsaji'. Take means; refuse the theology that death of martyrs proves the path wrong. Purify the heart when tests expose its contents.

Knowing of the breasts — the tenth time the surah has pressed inner knowledge. Hide less. Be of the faction of sakinah. Leave the thought of ignorance when the ummah bleeds.

=== 155 ===
"Indeed, those of you who turned back on the day the two hosts met — it was Satan who caused them to slip because of some of what they had earned. But Allah has already forgiven them. Indeed, Allah is Forgiving and Forbearing."

Those who turned the day the hosts met — Satan made them slip for some of what they had earned of sins. Allah has forgiven them. Forgiving, Forbearing. Honest: a slip linked to prior earnings, shaytanic push, then divine maghfirah for the believers among them. Not every runner at Uhud became a permanent villain; forgiveness is announced in revelation.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, welcomed back those who had fled when they returned in tawbah, and Uthman's story among others shows the complexity of that day. He taught that shaytan runs in the children of Adam like blood, and that sins open doors to slips in crises. Forbearing — Halim — means He does not rush to punish every failure of an otherwise believing heart.

Because of what they earned — personal responsibility under the shaytanic slip. Clean the earnings if you want fewer slips in the next host-meeting of your life. Forgiveness already given is not a story to make light of flight; it is oxygen for those who return.

If you have fled a duty, do not live in permanent self-damnation if you are returning; and do not cite this ayah to flee again. Halim and Ghafur want you firm next time. Shaytan still wants the slip. Choose the names you stand under.

Communities: when members fail under pressure then return, practice this ayah's ending — forgiveness — after accountability. A community that never forgives believers' past slips will run out of soldiers before the next Uhud.

The autopsy of Uhud is merciful and severe: promise fulfilled, then failure, dispute, and disobedience after seeing what was loved; mixed desires of dunya and akhirah; distress upon distress; sakinah on some and Jahiliyyah thought on others; shaytanic slip because of what was earned; forgiveness already announced for believers who return. The Prophet called from behind while some climbed away in panic, then led the wounded out again when the next call came.

When fear-mongers say the people have gathered, say hasbunallahu wa ni'ma al-wakil and let iman increase. Softness of the leader, shura, decision, and tawakkul — 3:159 — remain the pipeline of governance after every wound.

=== 156 ===
"O you who believe, do not be like those who disbelieved and said about their brothers when they traveled through the land or went out to fight, 'If they had been with us, they would not have died or been killed,' so Allah makes that a regret in their hearts. And it is Allah who gives life and causes death, and Allah is Seeing of what you do."

Do not copy the kafir speech about brothers who die on travel or jihad: if they had stayed with us they would not have died. Allah makes that speech a hasrah in their hearts — a regret that eats them. He gives life and death; He is basir of what you do. Qadr of death vs. the illusion of total human control — the line between iman and Jahiliyyah thought.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, forbade the wailing customs that imply death is pure waste and taught istirja' and sabr. He said the martyr is not dead in the sense of 2:154 and 3:169. Saying "if only" to cancel qadr is a phrase he disliked in the context of contending with decree after the fact while still ordering careful planning before.

Hasrah in hearts is the punishment of that speech already in dunya — a mental prison of counterfactuals. Believers grieve but do not build theology of blame against the decree. Allah gives life and death — close the counterfactual market.

When a brother dies in a crash or a lawful struggle, refuse the circle that says "if he had listened to us." Plan better next time if planning failed; do not speak like those who disbelieved. Basir watches the speech at funerals.

Train your tongue now: inna lillahi wa inna ilayhi raji'un; Allah takes and gives; we take means and submit. That training prevents the hasrah-speech from becoming your default under shock.

The autopsy of Uhud is merciful and severe: promise fulfilled, then failure, dispute, and disobedience after seeing what was loved; mixed desires of dunya and akhirah; distress upon distress; sakinah on some and Jahiliyyah thought on others; shaytanic slip because of what was earned; forgiveness already announced for believers who return. The Prophet called from behind while some climbed away in panic, then led the wounded out again when the next call came.

Softness of the leader, shura, decision, and tawakkul — 3:159 — remain the pipeline of governance after every wound.

=== 157 ===
"And if you are killed in the cause of Allah or die — forgiveness from Allah and mercy are better than whatever they accumulate."

If you are killed fi sabilillah or die, maghfirah and rahmah from Allah are better than what they gather of dunya. Death in His path and ordinary death of a believer both sit under a hope larger than accumulation. The "they" who accumulate are those who stayed behind counting spoils and years.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, said the martyr is forgiven at the first gush of blood, and he said when a believer's child dies and he seeks reward, he is given a house of praise in Paradise. Forgiveness and mercy better than jam' — piles of wealth — is the scale that makes Uhud's graves richer than Makkah's warehouses of the time.

Better is the key word. Measure careers and risk by it. What they accumulate looks larger in the market; maghfirah and rahmah are larger in truth. Choose the better when the two compete.

Live so that if death finds you in a lawful path of Allah, this ayah is your headline. And if death finds you in bed, still let maghfirah and rahmah be what you sought more than accumulation. The verse elevates both endings when the life before them was aimed at Him.

When envy of the rich living hits you at a funeral of a poor pious person, recite this ayah. Better than what they accumulate. Wipe the envy. Pray for maghfirah for the dead and for yourself.

The autopsy of Uhud is merciful and severe: promise fulfilled, then failure, dispute, and disobedience after seeing what was loved; mixed desires of dunya and akhirah; distress upon distress; sakinah on some and Jahiliyyah thought on others; shaytanic slip because of what was earned; forgiveness already announced for believers who return. The Prophet called from behind while some climbed away in panic, then led the wounded out again when the next call came.

Softness of the leader, shura, decision, and tawakkul — 3:159 — remain the pipeline of governance after every wound.

=== 158 ===
"And whether you die or are killed, unto Allah you will be gathered."

Die or be killed — ila Allahi tuhsharun. Gathering is the constant under every mode of death. Flight does not cancel hashr; shahadah does not cancel hashr; bed-death does not cancel hashr. All roads end in one square before Him. Uhud's variety of endings is unified by this destination.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, described the gathering barefoot and naked of worldly markers, and he taught living for the day of presentation. He said act, for each will be eased toward what he was created for. Gathered to Allah is either terror or longing depending on what you send ahead.

Let this short ayah end arguments about which death is "better" in a spirit of competition; both types gather. Prepare the gathering, not the headline of how you exited. Means of death are qadr; state of the heart is your pursuit.

Say when you hear of any death: unto Allah we are gathered. Then ask what you have prepared for that square. The verse is small enough to remember at every janazah and large enough to reorder a life.

Hashr kills both arrogance of the survivor and despair of the wounded. Survivors will be gathered; martyrs will be gathered. The difference is what each carries into the crowd of souls.

The autopsy of Uhud is merciful and severe: promise fulfilled, then failure, dispute, and disobedience after seeing what was loved; mixed desires of dunya and akhirah; distress upon distress; sakinah on some and Jahiliyyah thought on others; shaytanic slip because of what was earned; forgiveness already announced for believers who return. The Prophet called from behind while some climbed away in panic, then led the wounded out again when the next call came.

=== 159 ===
"So by mercy from Allah, you were lenient with them. And if you had been rude and harsh in heart, they would have disbanded from about you. So pardon them and ask forgiveness for them and consult them in the matter. And when you have decided, then rely upon Allah. Indeed, Allah loves those who rely upon Him."

The governance ayah after Uhud. By rahmah of Allah the Prophet was lin — soft — with them. If he had been fazz, harsh of speech, and ghaliz al-qalb, hard-hearted, they would have scattered from around him. So pardon, seek forgiveness for them, and consult them in the amr. When determined, tawakkal on Allah. He loves the mutawakkilin. Softness, pardon, istighfar for the flock, shura, decision, tawakkul — a full pipeline of leadership after failure.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, was the living tafsir: he did not expel every runner permanently; he asked for them forgiveness; he continued consultation as he had before Badr and Uhud; then he decided and relied — including the march to Hamra' al-Asad. He said the one who does not show mercy is not shown mercy. He loved shura and practised it though revelation came to him.

Leaders of homes and organisations: harsh hearts empty rooms. Softness is not absence of decision — the ayah ends in decision and tawakkul. Pardon what harmed you personally; ask Allah to forgive them; consult; decide; rely. Skipping to harshness loses the people you needed for the next battle.

Allah loves those who rely — so complete the chain. Shura without tawakkul is endless meetings; tawakkul without shura can be ego dressed as faith. The Prophet joined both under mercy.

If you are a follower who failed a leader, this ayah also shows you were pardoned in the Prophetic model — so do not fail again lightly. If you lead, print this ayah where you plan. Mercy first. Scatter is what harshness buys. Love of Allah is what tawakkul buys.

The autopsy of Uhud is merciful and severe: promise fulfilled, then failure, dispute, and disobedience after seeing what was loved; mixed desires of dunya and akhirah; distress upon distress; sakinah on some and Jahiliyyah thought on others; shaytanic slip because of what was earned; forgiveness already announced for believers who return. The Prophet called from behind while some climbed away in panic, then led the wounded out again when the next call came.

Softness of the leader, shura, decision, and tawakkul — 3:159 — remain the pipeline of governance after every wound.

=== 160 ===
"If Allah helps you, none can overcome you; but if He forsakes you, who is there who can help you after Him? And upon Allah let the believers rely."

If Allah helps you, none overcomes you. If He abandons you — yakhlukum — who helps you after Him? Upon Allah let believers rely. The fortress pair with 3:150. Help is decisive; abandonment cannot be patched by coalitions. Tawakkul is the only rational policy after that logic.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, lived both sides of the conditional in human experience of means — Badr's help, Uhud's teaching moment — while knowing Allah did not abandon the believers in the sense of canceling the deen. Khudhlan as withdrawal of special help is a warning to keep the conditions of aid: sabr, taqwa, obedience. He said know that if the ummah gathered to help you they could not help you except with what Allah wrote.

Stop collecting protectors as if they replace Him. Collect taqwa that invites His help. When help comes, do not attribute it to your genius. When help seems withdrawn, examine disobedience before you examine alliances only.

Believers rely on Him — not as a slogan after the plan, but as a frame before and after the plan. None can overcome you under His help: walk into just causes with that courage. Who helps after Him if He forsakes: walk with that humility.

Recite both conditionals in Fajr on hard days. Help; none overcome. Forsake; no helper. Tawakkul. Then move your feet as a believer who saw Uhud and still believes the fortress stands.

The autopsy of Uhud is merciful and severe: promise fulfilled, then failure, dispute, and disobedience after seeing what was loved; mixed desires of dunya and akhirah; distress upon distress; sakinah on some and Jahiliyyah thought on others; shaytanic slip because of what was earned; forgiveness already announced for believers who return. The Prophet called from behind while some climbed away in panic, then led the wounded out again when the next call came.

Do not hide communal failure; autopsy it. Do not treat fadl as licence to repeat the same disobedience. After injury, respond. When fear-mongers say the people have gathered, say hasbunallahu wa ni'ma al-wakil and let iman increase. Softness of the leader, shura, decision, and tawakkul — 3:159 — remain the pipeline of governance after every wound.

=== 161 ===
"And it is not for a prophet to act dishonestly with the spoils. And whoever defrauds will bring what he obtained by fraud on the Day of Resurrection. Then every soul will be paid in full what it earned, and they will not be wronged."

A prophet does not ghul — steal from spoils or hide war gains. Whoever does ghul brings what he took on the Day as a public burden. Every soul is paid fully what it earned; no zulm. Reports link this to purification of the army's ethics after battle when suspicion or temptation about distribution could arise. Revelation clears the Prophet and warns every hand around the spoils.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, said he would not take a needle or thread beyond his right, and he cursed the one who steals from the spoils. He said a man will come on the Day with a camel bellowing or a sheep bleating that he had stolen from the war gains. Full payment without wrong kills the fantasy of hidden enrichment in the name of jihad or of any public fund.

Apply beyond the battlefield: public money, zakat funds, NGO accounts, family estates. Ghul is betrayal of trust. Bringing it on the Day means the secret becomes a spectacle. Do not take what is not yours from the "spoils" of office.

Every soul paid what it earned — the same justice for the thief of a thread and the spender of a fortune. Not wronged means the poor soldier will not lose a coin of his right, and the thief will not keep a coin of his theft.

Leaders: publish distributions. Followers: do not accuse prophets of ghul without right — this ayah forbids that slander of Muhammad. Hands clean; tongues clean. Full payment is coming.

Ethics after battle, the favour of the Messenger, the sorting of believers and hypocrites, the life of the shuhada, and the refusal of counterfactual speech about the dead fill this passage. The Prophet cleared his hands of ghul and taught that death is by decree at an appointed time. Those killed in the path are alive with their Lord; the sitters who say "if they had obeyed us" are dared to avert death from themselves.

When a brother dies in a lawful struggle or on a journey of good, refuse the couch theology that blames qadr as bad planning. Prepare for gathering to Allah whether by bed or by field. If Allah helps you, none overcome you; if He forsakes you, no helper remains — so rely on Him after you take the means He loves.

=== 162 ===
"So is one who pursues the pleasure of Allah like one who brings upon himself the anger of Allah and whose refuge is Hell? And wretched is the destination."

Is the seeker of ridwan Allah like the one who returns with sakhat — anger — of Allah and whose mathwa is Hell? Wretched destination. Two paths after Uhud's ethics: pleasure of Allah vs anger of Allah. No moral equivalence. Spoils honesty, sabr, obedience — or fraud, flight, hypocrisy — end in opposite homes.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, taught seeking ridwan in the famous du'a after prayer and said Allah's pleasure is the greatest gift of Paradise. He warned of deeds that earn anger. The rhetorical is — a-fa-mani — expects a no. Do not live as if the two are similar lifestyles with similar outcomes.

Check which way your day aims. Ridwan or sakhat. Hell as refuge is the Quran's bitter irony for those who took the Fire as home. Wretched is the bed.

When someone glamorizes a haram lifestyle next to a path of taqwa as "just choices," recite this ayah. Not alike. Destination wretched vs pleasure of Allah. Choose with the end in view.

Say often: Allahumma inni as'aluka ridak. Seeking pleasure actively is the opposite of drifting into anger by neglect. Drift is a choice too.

Ethics after battle, the favour of the Messenger, the sorting of believers and hypocrites, the life of the shuhada, and the refusal of counterfactual speech about the dead fill this passage. === 163 ===
"They have degrees with Allah, and Allah is Seeing of what they do."

Hum — ranks, degrees — with Allah. He is basir of what they do. Not all seekers of ridwan are identical in rank; not all who fall are identical in blame. Degrees under one Seeing. Uhud produced shuhada of the highest ranks and runners who were forgiven — different hum.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, said the people of Paradise will see those above them as you see a bright star, and he said Allah has ninety-nine ranks for those who struggle in His path. Seeing of what they do means ranks track deeds, not slogans or length of beard alone.

Compete upward without envy that turns into hatred. Someone else's higher degree need not lower you unless you respond with baghy. Basir watches the competition's adab.

Ask for a high degree, then do the work. Do not claim a degree you have not earned. Seeing of deeds is the ranking committee — no lobbying.

Comfort for the unknown sincere: degrees with Allah may invert dunya fame. The unseen giver may outrank the famous speaker. Basir is not fooled by microphones.

Revelation that never becomes a step is how earlier communities held scripture and still went astray. Ask Allah to make this verse a proof for you, not against you, and to fix your heart on the path until you die upon islam.

=== 164 ===
"Certainly did Allah confer favour upon the believers when He sent among them a Messenger from themselves, reciting to them His verses and purifying them and teaching them the Book and wisdom, although they had been before in clear error."

Allah manna — great favour — on the believers by sending a messenger from themselves: tilawah, tazkiyah, ta'lim of Book and hikmah. Before that they were in clear dalal. The same four tasks of Ibrahim's du'a (2:129) and 2:151 return as completed favour. Uhud's hard lesson sits inside a larger gift: you have a messenger who recites, purifies, teaches. Error was your past; do not return to it by heels-turning.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, said I was sent to perfect good character, and he taught by living. From themselves — minkum — a human they knew, not an angel they could not imitate. Clear error before him in Arabia was shirk, burial of daughters, riba, tribal murder. Favour is measured by the darkness before the lamp.

Gratitude for the messenger is obedience to what he recited and taught. People who praise him while refusing the Book and hikmah refuse the favour's content. Tazkiyah means your character should be cleaner than the Jahiliyyah you came from — personal or modern.

Remember your own "before" — personal dalal before guidance — and the favour of whoever taught you. Then become a small lamp of tilawah, tazkiyah, and ta'lim for someone still in error.

Mann of Allah with the messenger is the greatest gift after tawhid itself. Do not let Uhud's pain make you forget Badr's and the whole mission's light. Favour first; training second; both from Him.

=== 165 ===
"Why is it that when a disaster struck you, although you had struck them with a disaster twice as great, you said, 'From where is this?' Say, 'It is from yourselves.' Indeed, Allah is over all things competent."

When a musibah hit you at Uhud — though you had hit them twice as hard at Badr (in numbers of fallen) — you said anna hadha, from where is this? Say: it is from yourselves. Allah is qadir over everything. The question of shock is answered with self-responsibility under qadr. Disobedience of the archers, desire of dunya, dispute — from yourselves. Competence of Allah means He is able to give Badr and Uhud for His aims.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, taught that no fatigue or thorn afflicts a believer without expiation, and he still linked communal outcomes to communal obedience. "From yourselves" does not cancel qadr; it names the sins that were the occasion of the teaching wound. He forbade the cry of those who only accuse the decree to escape repentance.

When disaster hits after a season of Muslim wrong, ask first about ourselves. From yourselves is bitter medicine that heals. Competence of Allah means He could have shielded you; He chose a lesson. Learn it.

Personal: from where is this illness, this loss? Qadr of Allah; and sometimes from your own door left open. Close the door with tawbah. Do not only scream anna hadha at the sky.

Twice as great — remember your wins when you count losses. Badr's double still stands in the ledger. Despair that forgets prior fadl is part of what "from yourselves" includes: ingratitude.

=== 166 ===
"And what struck you on the day the two armies met was by permission of Allah that He might make evident the believers."

What hit you the day the hosts met was bi-idhnillah so He might make evident the mu'minin. Permission frames the wound; making evident frames the purpose. Believers become visible under fire — to themselves, to the community, to history. Hidden claims of iman open on the field.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, said people are like mines of silver and gold; the best in Jahiliyyah are the best in Islam when they understand. Making evident is excavation. He valued those who stood at Uhud after the panic as much as he honoured Badr's people in their own rank.

By permission — nothing random. So that He make evident — nothing purposeless. Hold both when the day of the two armies is a metaphor for your crisis. Your iman is being published. Write something worth reading.

Do not envy those who were evident as firm that day; become evident in your day. Permission of Allah under your wound is the same permission under their firmness. Ask for a beautiful appearance of your faith when the hosts meet.

Community archives should remember the firm of Uhud and the forgiven of Uhud honestly — both made evident. Myths of a flawless generation help no one; this ayah prefers evidence over myth.

=== 167 ===
"And that He might make evident the hypocrites. And it was said to them, 'Come, fight in the way of Allah or defend.' They said, 'If we had known fighting, we would have followed you.' That day they were nearer to disbelief than to belief, saying with their mouths what was not in their hearts. And Allah is most Knowing of what they conceal."

Purpose continues: make evident the munafiqin. They were told come fight fi sabilillah or at least defend — they said if we knew there would be fighting we would have followed you. That day they were closer to kufr than iman, saying with mouths what was not in hearts. He knows what they hide. Abdullah ibn Ubayy's withdrawal with about three hundred is the famous occasion. Mock ignorance of fighting was a mask; the heart had already chosen safety over the Prophet's call.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, knew them and often did not expose every name, leaving knowledge of the concealed to Allah while protecting the community. He said the signs of the munafiq include lying, promise-breaking, and betrayal when trusted. Mouth vs heart is the disease. Closer to kufr than iman is a terrifying proximity measure for a double life.

When the call to defend the deen comes and you invent "if we had known," check the heart. Concealment is already known to Allah. Making evident may come as a day that forces your mouth and heart into the open.

Do not use accusations of nifaq lightly against Muslims in ordinary disputes. Do use this ayah to audit yourself when duty calls and your tongue makes excuses your chest does not believe. He knows what you conceal — the tenth reminder of inner knowledge in this surah's logic.

Defend — dafi'u — even without full offensive jihad was offered as a minimum. They refused even that. Minimums refused are evidence. When you refuse even the minimum of a duty, you write your own proximity to the wrong side.

=== 168 ===
"Those who said about their brothers while sitting, 'If they had obeyed us, they would not have been killed.' Say, 'Then avert death from yourselves, if you should be truthful.'"

The sitters say of their brothers who were killed: if they had obeyed us they would not have been killed. Say: then push death away from yourselves if you are truthful. The challenge destroys the counterfactual arrogance of those who stayed behind. If control of death is yours, start with your own soul. You cannot. So stop speaking as if the martyrs died of foolishness rather than of qadr on a path of honour.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, buried the shuhada and praised them, and he forbade the soft poison of "if only" that undermines sacrifice. He taught the words of istirja' and the belief that death has an appointed term. Sitting + accusing the dead is a double cowardice this ayah shames.

Avert death from yourselves — a dare. Every funeral of a person who took a risk for good will attract this speech from the couch. Answer with the Quran's dare. Truthful people would demonstrate control of death before advising others to fear it more than Allah.

When you catch "if they had listened to me" in your mouth at a funeral of a mujahid or a truth-teller, swallow it. Replace with du'a and with the admission of qadr. Sitting is already a choice; do not salt it with insult of those who stood.

Say the ayah to the inner self that loves to be wise after the event. Then get up from the couch for the next duty while you still can. Death will not be averted by your commentary.

=== 169 ===
"And never think of those who have been killed in the way of Allah as dead. Rather, they are alive with their Lord, receiving provision."

Do not think the shuhada dead. They are alive with their Lord, yurzaqun — provided for. The same teaching as 2:154, deepened after Uhud's graves. Life with Allah is real; provision is real; your "dead" is a misreading of eyes that only track breath in the chest. The Prophet, peace be upon him, said their souls are in green birds in Paradise, and he said the martyr wants to return because of what he sees of honour.

Grief remains allowed — he wept — but theology of waste is forbidden. Alive with their Lord rewrites the funeral. Provision rewrites the fear that they lost the world and gained nothing.

Never think — la tahsabanna — is an order to the living's imagination. Train imagination with revelation. When you visit Uhud's memory or any grave of someone killed fi sabilillah under rightful meaning, greet them as living with their Lord, not as ended stories.

This is not a licence for suicide or for every political death to be named shahadah without fiqh. It is comfort and honour for those truly killed in His path. Keep the term honest so the comfort stays honest.

Living believers: send them salam and sadaqah jariyah of du'a; envy their provision in a way that makes you braver, not reckless. Alive with their Lord — and you are still in the shop of deeds. Work.

=== 170 ===
"Rejoicing in what Allah has bestowed upon them of His bounty, and they receive good tidings about those after them who have not yet joined them — that there will be no fear concerning them, nor will they grieve."

The living-with-their-Lord are farihin — rejoicing — in Allah's fadl, and yastabshirun — glad — for those who have not yet joined them: no fear, no grief upon those coming. Martyrs care about who will follow; their bushra includes the ummah still on the road. No fear and no grief is the refrain of successful souls in the Quran.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, described their wish that those left behind know they are alive and well. He created a culture of loving the shuhada without neglecting the living. Glad tidings flow both ways when the community stays on the path.

No fear, no grief — aim your life at that pair. The martyrs already have it and want it for you. Join them in the long run by dying on iman; join their joy now by reducing fear and grief through tawakkul and taqwa.

When depressed about the ummah's state, imagine the shuhada's istibshar for those who still hold the rope. Do not cancel their gladness by quitting. Be among those they receive good tidings about.

Fadl they rejoice in is greater than the dunya they left. Measure your attachments again. What they have is what 3:15 promised the muttaqin — and more of nearness known only to them.

=== 171 ===
"They receive good tidings of favour from Allah and bounty and that Allah does not waste the reward of the believers."

More bushra: ni'mah, fadl, and that Allah does not waste ajr of the mu'minin. The theme of not denying good (3:115) returns for believers at large. Martyrs' gladness includes confidence in Allah's accounting for all who believe and work, not only those who died on the field.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, said Allah does not wrong anyone a speck and multiplies good. He said the believer's affair is all good. Waste of reward is a human fear this ayah kills. Keep working; the payroll is competent.

Believers whose deeds feel small next to shuhada still sit under "He does not waste." Ni'mah and fadl are wide. Do your lane. The glad tidings include you if you are of the mu'minin.

When shaytan says your secret good was wasted, recite this. When a project for deen seems to fail, recite this. Ajr is not the same as visible outcome. He does not waste.

Receive the bushra yourself: favour, bounty, preserved reward. Then give bushra to a tired worker of good in your community. The shuhada's joy is a culture the living should copy.

=== 172 ===
"Those who responded to Allah and the Messenger after injury had struck them — for those who did good among them and feared Allah is a great reward."

After the qarh of Uhud, some responded to Allah and the Messenger for the next march (Hamra' al-Asad). Those who did ihsan and had taqwa among them get ajr azim. Response after injury is a higher grade than enthusiasm before injury. Wounded people who still say labbayk are the ummah's gold.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, called them out the day after Uhud and only those who had been present went; their reward was announced in this revelation. He loved those who stood when pain said sit. Great reward matches the great difficulty of moving with wounds.

After your injury — illness, failure, betrayal — respond to the next call of duty. Do not build a permanent tent in the clinic of self-pity. Ihsan and taqwa condition the great reward; response alone with bad intention is not enough.

Community: when people are hurt, invite them again with mercy like 3:159, and honour those who come. Great reward is already written for wounded responders. Tell them so.

Personal inventory: what call have I refused since I was hurt? Answer it this week if it is still a right of Allah or His servants. Ajr azim prefers the wounded who walk.

=== 173 ===
"Those to whom hypocrites said, 'Indeed, the people have gathered against you, so fear them.' But it increased them in faith, and they said, 'Sufficient for us is Allah, and He is the best Disposer of affairs.'"

Hypocrites and hostile voices said: people have gathered against you — fear them. It increased the true in iman, and they said hasbunallahu wa ni'ma al-wakil — Allah is enough for us and the best Disposer. Fear propaganda became fuel for faith. The slogan of Ibrahim when thrown to the fire, in the hadith, returns on the tongues of Uhud's responders.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, said this phrase when he was told of gathered enemies, and he taught it as a refuge. He said when you see something that grieves you, say hasbunallahu wa ni'ma al-wakil. Increase in iman under threat is the opposite of nifaq's increase in excuses.

When media or local voices say "they have gathered, fear," answer with the phrase and with preparation. Enough is Allah; best disposer is He. Fear them is the command of the hypocrite; fear Allah is the command of the Book.

Teach children hasbunallahu wa ni'ma al-wakil before they meet the first bully of ideology. Increase in iman under pressure is trainable. Start with the tongue's truth and the heart's repetition.

Gathered people are many; the Wakil is one and enough. Do not count only their gathering. Count your disposer. The ayah made a weapon out of a threat — copy the alchemy.

=== 174 ===
"So they returned with favour from Allah and bounty, no harm having touched them. And they pursued the pleasure of Allah, and Allah is the possessor of great bounty."

They returned with ni'mah and fadl, no su' touching them, having followed ridwan Allah. Dhu fadl 'azim. The Hamra' al-Asad march ended without the harm the fear-mongers promised. Seeking Allah's pleasure flipped the threat into a story of safe return with bounty. Great bounty is Allah's always; sometimes it appears as a wound avoided after courage.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, led them out wounded and returned them under this description. He taught that with hardship comes ease and that tawakkul plus means is the sunnah. No harm touched them — that time. Other times harm touches and still fadl remains. Both are Quran.

Pursue ridwan even when the crowd says fear. Return is with Allah's favour when He wills. Do not demand this exact outcome every time; demand of yourself this exact orientation every time.

Great bounty — if you were protected after a brave step, name it fadl, not luck. If you were hit, name the hit with sabr and still name Him dhu fadl 'azim. Orientation over outcome-control.

Ajr of those who go out after injury includes the safe return stories and the martyrdom stories. This ayah is one branch. Take the branch of ridwan and leave the fruiting to Him.

=== 175 ===
"That is only Satan who frightens his allies. So fear them not, but fear Me, if you are believers."

Innama dhalikum al-shaytan — that fear-mongering is only Satan scaring his awliya. So do not fear them; fear Me, if you are mu'minin. The gathered-enemy speech is given a source: shaytan. His method is takhwif, frightening. The response is a swap of fears. Believers are defined by whom they fear more.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, sought refuge from cowardice and from shaytan's whispers, and he taught that shaytan runs on the veins. He said the strong is the one who controls himself. Fear of people is a shackle he broke in companions until old women in Makkah no longer defined their salah times.

If you are believers — condition — fear Me not them. Audit your decisions: which fear wrote them? Fear of mockery, of poverty, of the gathered? Or fear of Allah? Swap. Shaytan frightens his allies — if their fear controls you, you are orbiting the wrong wilayah.

When a headline is designed to terrify Muslims into silence, name it: takhwif of shaytan. Then fear Allah and speak the truth as needed. Allies of shaytan want your fear; do not feed them.

Practice: when fear of people rises, say a'udhu billahi min al-shaytan al-rajim and hasbunallah. Then do the small act you feared. Muscles of the right fear grow by use.

=== 176 ===
"And do not be grieved by those who hasten into disbelief. Indeed, they will never harm Allah at all. Allah intends that He should give them no share in the Hereafter, and for them is a great punishment."

Do not let those who race into kufr grieve you, O Prophet — and every caller after. They do not harm Allah at all. He intends to make no share for them in the akhirah; great punishment is theirs. The Prophet's grief for rejecters is real in the sirah; this ayah bounds it so it does not break him. No share in the Hereafter is the true loss; their speed into kufr is not a victory over God.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, nearly destroyed himself with grief for their disbelief (18:6) until revelation trained the grief. He still made da'wah until death. Not grieving means not despairing; it does not mean not caring. No harm to Allah — Ghani — frees the caller from thinking a famous apostate has injured the throne.

When public figures race to kufr and cameras love them, do not let your chest collapse. No share in the akhirah is a terrifying empty bank. Great punishment waits. Your job is conveyance and a firm heart.

Race into disbelief is a dark mirror of racing into forgiveness (3:133). People race somewhere. Invite them to the right race while refusing grief that paralyses. Allah is not harmed; you need not be destroyed.

Make du'a for the guidance of the racing rejecter if you can, as Ibrahim prayed for his father until forbidden — and stop where revelation stops. Boundaries of grief and du'a are part of sunnah.

=== 177 ===
"Indeed, those who purchase disbelief at the price of faith will never harm Allah at all, and for them is a painful punishment."

They buy kufr with iman as the price — a trade already condemned in al-Baqarah's language of bad purchases. They never harm Allah at all; painful punishment is theirs. The trade metaphor makes apostasy look like a market decision with a catastrophic receipt.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, said the bankrupt is the one who loses his deeds in the market of wronging people; here the bankrupt loses iman itself for kufr's glitter. No harm to Allah repeats to kill any romance of "striking a blow against the system" by leaving Islam. The throne does not shake; the trader does.

If you are tempted to sell iman for a job, a partner, a passport of praise, read the receipt: painful punishment, no harm to Him, total loss for you. Cancel the trade while the ink is wet.

Purchase language means you can refuse the sale. Agency remains. Painful punishment means the product is poison. Never harm Allah means your exit is not a trophy against heaven.

Community: when someone sells iman, hold the door of tawbah open without treating the sale as harmless lifestyle. Both mercy and gravity. The ayah supplies gravity; 3:89 supplies door.

Ask Allah to make this verse a proof for you, not against you, and to fix your heart on the path until you die upon islam.

=== 178 ===
"And let not those who disbelieve think that Our respite to them is better for them. We only give them respite that they may increase in sin, and for them is a humiliating punishment."

Do not let disbelievers think that Our delaying them is khayr for them. We only delay them so they may increase ithm; for them is a degrading punishment. Imla' — respite — is not approval. Long life of a tyrant can be rope to increase sin, not a prize. Humiliating punishment ends the illusion.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, said Allah gives respite to the zalim until He seizes him and he does not escape. He said if you see Allah giving a servant what he loves while the servant persists in sin, that is istidraj — gradual lure to ruin. Respite that increases sin is a terrifying gift.

When you envy a disbelieving oppressor's long health and wealth, recite this ayah. Better for them? No. Increase in sin. Humiliating punishment. Envy dies. Fear for them if you can still make du'a; fear the same pattern if you are a sinner given long rope.

Muslims with long ease in sin: check for istidraj. Respite is a chance for tawbah first; if you only increase, you may be reading this ayah from the inside. Turn before the seizure.

Humiliating — muhin — means the end strips the pride the respite inflated. Live so your end is honour under Allah, not muhin punishment after a long party of ithm.

Ethics after battle, the favour of the Messenger, the sorting of believers and hypocrites, the life of the shuhada, and the refusal of counterfactual speech about the dead fill this passage. The Prophet cleared his hands of ghul and taught that death is by decree at an appointed time. Those killed in the path are alive with their Lord; the sitters who say "if they had obeyed us" are dared to avert death from themselves.

When a brother dies in a lawful struggle or on a journey of good, refuse the couch theology that blames qadr as bad planning. Prepare for gathering to Allah whether by bed or by field. If Allah helps you, none overcome you; if He forsakes you, no helper remains — so rely on Him after you take the means He loves.

=== 179 ===
"Allah would not leave the believers in the state you are in until He distinguishes the bad from the good. Nor would Allah reveal to you the unseen. But Allah chooses of His messengers whom He wills. So believe in Allah and His messengers. And if you believe and fear Him, then for you is a great reward."

Allah will not leave you as you are until He separates the khabith from the tayyib. He will not give you all the ghayb; He chooses messengers and gives them what He wills of it. Believe in Allah and His messengers. If you believe and have taqwa, great reward. Uhud's mixing of ranks cannot be the permanent state; sorting continues. Curiosity about full unseen is refused; iman and taqwa are ordered instead.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, was given some ghayb and not all, and he said I am only a human. He forbade seeking the unseen through soothsayers. Choosing messengers kills the demand that every believer receive revelation. Sorting of bad and good is the ongoing sunnah of tests.

Do not demand to know every hypocrite by name or every future event. Demand of yourself to be tayyib in the sorting. Believe and fear — great reward. Leaving you mixed forever would be injustice to the pure; sorting is mercy even when it hurts.

When a test exposes someone you trusted as khabith, remember this ayah — He will not leave you mixed forever. When you fear you look mixed, purify before the next distinguishing wave.

Great reward for iman plus taqwa is the stable offer while ghayb stays restricted. Take the offer. Leave the keys of the unseen with Him and His chosen messengers.

=== 180 ===
"And let not those who greedily withhold what Allah has given them of His bounty think that it is better for them. Rather, it is worse for them. Their necks will be encircled by what they withheld on the Day of Resurrection. And to Allah belongs the heritage of the heavens and the earth. And Allah is Acquainted with what you do."

Those who yabkhalun — stingily withhold — what Allah gave them of His fadl should not think it is good for them. It is worse. What they withheld will be their necklace-collar on the Day — reports speak of a serpent of wealth unpaid in zakah. Heritage of heavens and earth is Allah's; He is khabir of what you do. You cannot keep what He inherits anyway; bakhil only keeps the sin.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, said the example of the stingy and the giver is two men in armour; the giver's armour expands and the stingy's sticks to his neck. He said Allah receives sadaqah with His right hand and grows it. He warned that withholding zakah brings punishment of wealth itself. Collar of the withheld is a hadith image matching this ayah.

Worse for them — not better. Kill the thought that hoarding against Allah's right is prudence. Heritage is His — you are a temporary trustee. Khabir ends creative accounting.

Pay what is due: zakah, debts, rights of family. Give beyond duty from fadl. The neck on the Day will either be free or collared. Choose free now while gold can still move from hand to hand as sadaqah rather than as snake.

Allah's heritage of heavens and earth means every estate returns. Bakhils lose the wealth and keep the collar. Generous people lose the wealth (as all do at death) and keep the ajr. The math is simple once the Day is real to you.

=== 181 ===
"Allah has certainly heard the statement of those who said, 'Indeed, Allah is poor, while we are rich.' We will record what they said and their killing of the prophets without right and will say, 'Taste the punishment of the Burning Fire.'"

Allah heard those who said Allah is poor and we are rich — a vile mockery when asked to spend or when resenting the command of a generous God. He records what they said and their killing of prophets without right, and will say: taste the punishment of the burning. Speech against Allah's richness and blood of prophets sit in one file. Hearing is already done; recording is continuous; tasting is future.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, faced mockery of divine commands of spending and reported that Allah said: My servant has insulted Me and he should not; he said I have a son, and he said I am poor — meanings matching this cluster of insults. He taught Allah's name al-Ghani. Saying Allah is poor is among the ugliest jaahiliyyah of the wallet.

Recorded speech includes our jokes about "God needs my money" when zakah is mentioned. Kill that joke. Rich is He; poor is the one who refuses to spend for Him. Burning is the end of the mockery.

Killing prophets without right joins the file — the surah will not let economic insult be separated from historical blood. Contempt for Allah's command and contempt for Allah's messengers are relatives.

Taste — the sensory verb again. They tasted power in speech; they will taste fire in consequence. Hear, record, taste: three stages. You are still in the stage where hearing can lead to tawbah before tasting.

The surah closes with hearing of ugly speech against Allah, the certainty of death, tests in wealth and selves, hurtful tongues, the covenant to clarify scripture, cosmic signs for those of understanding, and the long Rabbana of people who remember Allah in every posture. He answered them: no work of male or female is wasted; hijrah, harm, and struggle are met with expiation and gardens.

Do not be deceived by the free movement of disbelievers in the land; it is brief; Hell is a wretched bed; the muttaqin have lodging from Allah. Among the People of the Book are humble believers who do not sell ayat. Final order: patient, outlast, remain stationed, fear Allah — that you may succeed. Keep the four verbs after you close the mushaf.

=== 182 ===
"That is for what your hands have put forth and because Allah is not ever unjust to the servants."

That punishment is for what your hands put forth — and because Allah is never zalim to the servants. Hands as symbol of earned deeds. No injustice in the Fire for those who mocked and killed. The seal of justice under every severe ayah returns.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, said Allah has forbidden zulm on Himself and between you. He said every wrong will be settled. Hands put forth is Quranic idiom for responsibility (2:95, 18:57). What you send forward arrives.

When you fear a harsh ending, ask what your hands have sent. When you see a harsh ending threatened to oppressors, know it is not zulm from Allah. Both directions of the ayah train the soul.

Put forth good with the hands: charity, restrained anger, written beneficial knowledge, a handshake of peace. Hands are still free to change the file before the tasting.

Allah is not unjust to the servants — including you if you are oppressed. Your file of wounds is not ignored. Hands of the oppressor put forth; justice will match them. Patience until then is not foolishness.

The surah closes with hearing of ugly speech against Allah, the certainty of death, tests in wealth and selves, hurtful tongues, the covenant to clarify scripture, cosmic signs for those of understanding, and the long Rabbana of people who remember Allah in every posture. He answered them: no work of male or female is wasted; hijrah, harm, and struggle are met with expiation and gardens.

Among the People of the Book are humble believers who do not sell ayat. Final order: patient, outlast, remain stationed, fear Allah — that you may succeed. Keep the four verbs after you close the mushaf.

Cross this ayah with its neighbours so the thread stays tight — Al Imran rarely drops an isolated brick. The Prophet, peace be upon him, taught that deeds are by intentions and by endings, and that Allah looks at hearts and actions. Read, then move one limb: a tawbah, a payment of right, a held tongue, a posted duty you stop abandoning. Revelation that never becomes a step is how earlier communities held scripture and still went astray. === 183 ===
"Those who said, 'Indeed, Allah has taken our promise not to believe any messenger until he brings us an offering which fire consumes.' Say, 'There have already come to you messengers before me with clear proofs and with that of which you speak. So why did you kill them, if you should be truthful?'"

They claim a covenant with Allah not to believe a messenger until he brings a qurbani consumed by fire — a demand styled as piety. Say: messengers before me came with bayyinat and with what you mention — so why did you kill them if you are truthful? The demand is unmasked as a stalling tactic by the history of killing prophets who already matched their conditions. Moving goalposts after murder is not sincerity.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, refused to become a court magician for every demanded sign, and the Quran says signs only increase some people in aversion (17:59). He brought the Quran as the living sign. Why did you kill them — the question that ends the alibi.

Modern moving goalposts: "I will believe if X miracle," then X comes and another X is demanded, or the history of ignored proofs is forgotten. Truthful people would have accepted earlier messengers who already fit. Stalling is a known game.

If you demand signs, audit whether you obeyed the signs already on your shelf — Quran, creation, the moral law in your chest. Killing prophets is the extreme of rejection; slow neglect of the Book is a soft cousin. Do not be a cousin of the stallers.

Say to the self that always wants one more proof before fajr becomes regular: messengers and proofs already came. Why do you "kill" the daily command by delay if you are truthful about wanting truth?

The surah closes with hearing of ugly speech against Allah, the certainty of death, tests in wealth and selves, hurtful tongues, the covenant to clarify scripture, cosmic signs for those of understanding, and the long Rabbana of people who remember Allah in every posture. Keep the four verbs after you close the mushaf.

=== 184 ===
"Then if they deny you, messengers before you were denied who brought clear proofs and written ordinances and the enlightening Scripture."

If they deny you, O Muhammad, they denied messengers before who brought bayyinat, zubur, and the illuminating kitab. You are not the first rejected bearer of light. Continuity of rejection comforts the final messenger and every later caller. Denial is a pattern of the diseased heart, not a proof against the message.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, said the prophets of Banu Israel were many and many were denied and killed, and he told Nuh's long centuries of denial. He kept conveying. Written ordinances and illuminating scripture still got rejected by some — so do not think a perfect pamphlet alone converts a sealed chest.

When your da'wah is denied, join the caravan of denied messengers in patience, not in conspiracy self-flattery only. Check your manners; then, if the message was clear, take the ayah's comfort. Denial happened to better people than you with brighter proofs.

Illuminating scripture still requires open eyes. You can hold a lamp to a sleeper; you cannot force the lids. Convey; leave guidance to Allah; do not burn out because of a pattern as old as Nuh.

Study the stories of denied prophets when you feel unique in rejection. The Quran gave you a cloud of witnesses who were denied first. Walk. Recite. Do not stop the tilawah because the ears are hard.

The surah closes with hearing of ugly speech against Allah, the certainty of death, tests in wealth and selves, hurtful tongues, the covenant to clarify scripture, cosmic signs for those of understanding, and the long Rabbana of people who remember Allah in every posture. Keep the four verbs after you close the mushaf.

=== 185 ===
"Every soul will taste death, and you will only be given your full compensation on the Day of Resurrection. So he who is drawn away from the Fire and admitted to Paradise has attained success. And what is the life of this world except the enjoyment of delusion."

Every soul tastes death. Full wages only on the Day of Rising. Whoever is moved away from the Fire and entered into jannah has succeeded — faza. The life of this world is only mata' al-ghurur — enjoyment of delusion. Uhud's deaths sit under a universal law: every soul, not only the fallen on the hill. Success is redefined as escape from Fire and entry to Paradise, not as surviving the battle or dying with the most spoils. Dunya is a deceptive pleasure — real taste, false permanence.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, said remember often the destroyer of pleasures — death. He said the smart is the one who takes himself to account and works for after death. He described Paradise and Fire until they were seen as if with eyes. Faza is the only success that matters at the end of every biography.

Taste of death is certain; full payment is deferred; success is the Fire-to-garden transfer; dunya is delusion of duration. Four truths. Organize life around them. Enjoy halal of dunya without drinking the ghurur that says it lasts.

When someone succeeds in dunya and fails the Fire test, do not call him successful. When someone fails in dunya and is drawn from the Fire, call him the winner. Language of fawz in the Quran should retrain your congratulations and condolences.

Mata' al-ghurur does not mean hate the world; it means do not trust its marketing. Use it as a bridge. Every soul will taste death — including the one reading this. Send the payment ahead before the taste arrives.

The surah closes with hearing of ugly speech against Allah, the certainty of death, tests in wealth and selves, hurtful tongues, the covenant to clarify scripture, cosmic signs for those of understanding, and the long Rabbana of people who remember Allah in every posture. He answered them: no work of male or female is wasted; hijrah, harm, and struggle are met with expiation and gardens.

Keep the four verbs after you close the mushaf.

=== 186 ===
"You will surely be tested in your wealth and your lives, and you will surely hear from those who were given the Scripture before you and from those who associate others with Allah much hurtful speech. But if you are patient and fear Allah — indeed, that is of the matters of determination."

You will be tested in wealth and selves — la-tublawunna. You will hear from People of the Book and from mushrikin much adha of speech. If you have sabr and taqwa, that is of 'azm al-umur — the matters of real resolve. Certainty of tests and of insult; condition of success is sabr plus taqwa. Uhud was one form; tongue-lashes are another; both promised.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, was insulted as magician, madman, poet, and he said Allah suffices me. He taught that the believer who mixes with people and is patient with their harm is better than the one who does not mix and is not patient. He said sabr is at the first shock. Resolve — 'azm — is a Quranic praise word for those who hold the line under insult.

Wealth tests by loss and by excess; selves by fear, illness, and death. Hurtful speech tests by humiliation. Prepare all three with sabr and taqwa. Muting every insult is not always possible; firmness under insult is.

When a cartoon, a politician, or a relative wounds with words about your deen, this ayah already booked the appointment. Keep the appointment with sabr and taqwa, not with haram revenge or with collapsed iman. 'Azm al-umur is a medal for that stance.

Train: small insults handled with calm for Allah's sake. Muscle grows. The big adha will come. Certainty of the verse means you need not be shocked — only ready.

The surah closes with hearing of ugly speech against Allah, the certainty of death, tests in wealth and selves, hurtful tongues, the covenant to clarify scripture, cosmic signs for those of understanding, and the long Rabbana of people who remember Allah in every posture. He answered them: no work of male or female is wasted; hijrah, harm, and struggle are met with expiation and gardens.

Do not be deceived by the free movement of disbelievers in the land; it is brief; Hell is a wretched bed; the muttaqin have lodging from Allah. Among the People of the Book are humble believers who do not sell ayat. Final order: patient, outlast, remain stationed, fear Allah — that you may succeed. Keep the four verbs after you close the mushaf.

=== 187 ===
"And when Allah took a covenant from those who were given the Scripture: 'You must make it clear to the people and not conceal it.' But they threw it away behind their backs and exchanged it for a small price. And wretched is that which they purchased."

Covenant on the People of the Book: explain the Book to people and do not conceal it. They tossed it behind their backs and bought a small price with it. Wretched purchase. Kitman again — the crime of 2:159 and 3:71 — under a taken mithaq. Throwing behind the back is the physical image of neglect and contempt. Small price is status, food, or faction power.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, said convey from me even a verse, and he warned of the bridle of fire for one who conceals knowledge. He left the Quran and sunnah openly taught. Wretched is what they purchased — the receipt of every scholar who buried a verse for a gift.

Muslims inherit the warning: make the Book clear; do not conceal. Teachers who hide unpopular ayat for popularity reenact the purchase. Small price still feels large in the moment; the ayah names it small and wretched from the Day's view.

Throwing behind the back can be personal: a mushaf you never open, a known ruling you never apply. Pick it up from behind the back. Put it in front of the eyes. The covenant's spirit wants light, not storage.

Wretched purchase — cancel the trade if you are in it. Publish the truth you buried. Return the small price if it was a bribe. The covenant is still the standard for anyone given a portion of scripture.

The surah closes with hearing of ugly speech against Allah, the certainty of death, tests in wealth and selves, hurtful tongues, the covenant to clarify scripture, cosmic signs for those of understanding, and the long Rabbana of people who remember Allah in every posture. Among the People of the Book are humble believers who do not sell ayat. Final order: patient, outlast, remain stationed, fear Allah — that you may succeed. Keep the four verbs after you close the mushaf.

=== 188 ===
"And never think that those who rejoice in what they have brought about and love to be praised for what they did not do — never think them secure from the punishment, and for them is a painful punishment."

Do not think that those who delight in their ugly deeds and love praise for what they did not do are safe from 'adhab. Painful punishment is theirs. Two diseases: joy in evil done, and love of credit for good not done. Both appear in politics, scholarship, and social media. Safety is an illusion the ayah cancels.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, said the one who claims what he was not given is like one wearing two garments of falsehood. He hated riya' and false reputation. He taught that deeds are by intentions — so stolen praise is a kind of theft plus lie. Rejoicing in evil is the opposite of the believer's regret and tawbah after slips.

Never think them secure — la tahsabanna — protects you from envying their PR success. Painful punishment is the unseen headline. Do not join their joy in evil; do not join their hunger for false praise.

Audit: do I celebrate a harm I caused? Do I love to be thanked for work others did? Both need tawbah. Security from punishment is not for that path.

In the age of curated feeds, this ayah is a daily friend. False praise is cheap to harvest and expensive on the Day. Real deeds in secret are expensive now and cheap to carry then — light on the back, heavy on the scale.

The surah closes with hearing of ugly speech against Allah, the certainty of death, tests in wealth and selves, hurtful tongues, the covenant to clarify scripture, cosmic signs for those of understanding, and the long Rabbana of people who remember Allah in every posture. He answered them: no work of male or female is wasted; hijrah, harm, and struggle are met with expiation and gardens.

Keep the four verbs after you close the mushaf.

=== 189 ===
"And to Allah belongs the dominion of the heavens and the earth, and Allah is over all things competent."

Mulk of heavens and earth is His; He is qadir over everything. The short seal under the long moral passage: power and ability belong to the One who records mockery, sorts ranks, takes life, and pays in full. Competence means no escape and no failed plan of His.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, praised Allah with these meanings in rukú' and sujud: lillahi al-mulk. He taught that if He wants something He says Be. Competence comforts the oppressed and warns the oppressor.

When the board of dunya looks owned by others, recite this. Mulk is His. When a problem looks unsolvable, recite this. Qadir over everything. Then take the next means.

Seal your night with it after news of chaos. Heavens and earth still His. Competence still intact. Your panic does not own the outcome; He does.

Pair with action: mulk is His, so spend from His property as a trustee; He is competent, so ask big in du'a. Theology without those two applications is only ink.

The surah closes with hearing of ugly speech against Allah, the certainty of death, tests in wealth and selves, hurtful tongues, the covenant to clarify scripture, cosmic signs for those of understanding, and the long Rabbana of people who remember Allah in every posture. === 190 ===
"Indeed, in the creation of the heavens and the earth and the alternation of the night and the day are signs for those of understanding."

Creation of heavens and earth and the flip of night and day are ayat for ulu al-albab — people of core intellect. The surah that argued law, war, and People of the Book ends its arc with cosmic signs for thinkers who remember. Intellect here is not arrogance; it is the soft core that sees through sky and time to the Creator.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, wept when these verses were revealed and stood the night with them, and he said woe to the one who reads them and does not reflect. He looked at the sky and recited. Alternation of night and day is a free university if the albab is alive.

Signs for understanding people — if you do not feel them, ask for a living core, not only more data. Science that measures creation without bowing is not the albab this ayah praises. Measurement plus sujud is.

Schedule reflection: five minutes under the sky without a phone. Night and day turn for you. Heavens hold without pillars you can see. Say rabbi ma khalaqta hadha batilan as the next ayah teaches.

Ulu al-albab become the speakers of the following du'as. Join them by thinking until dhikr starts, and making dhikr until thought deepens. Creation is the first book; the Quran is the clear book; together they guide.

The surah closes with hearing of ugly speech against Allah, the certainty of death, tests in wealth and selves, hurtful tongues, the covenant to clarify scripture, cosmic signs for those of understanding, and the long Rabbana of people who remember Allah in every posture.
